## Source: AGENTS.md

~~~~~~~~text
# explorEDA working rules

- Start with the [agent guide](docs/agent-guide.md): where code lives, the commands that work, and known traps.
- Keep this existing React library and demo in React and TypeScript.
- Use pnpm. Run `pnpm check` after a broad change; see [Verifying changes](#verifying-changes).
- Read [UI defaults](docs/ui-defaults.md) before changing controls, tables, overlays, or field details.
- Use `--border` for neutral borders, `--input` for inputs, and semantic tokens for status borders.
- Never add a `title` attribute or SVG `<title>` element to rendered UI. Both create native hover tooltips.
- Use `aria-label` or visible text for accessible names. Use `ActionTooltip` or the `Button` `tooltip` prop only when hover help is needed.
- Give every control whose effect is not obvious from its visible text a real tooltip that explains it: toggles, short or abbreviated labels, icon buttons, and options that differ subtly, such as Core and Full. Use `ActionTooltip` or the `Button` `tooltip` prop so it opens on hover and keyboard focus. Never fall back to a `title` attribute.
- Run `pnpm check:ui`; it rejects native tooltip sources in TSX.
- Keep user-facing copy about the product. Put internal caveats, release gaps, known bugs, and to-dos in the owning intent doc under `docs/intent/` or a GitHub issue, not on the page, in the UI, or in example comments.
- When shaping or planning an initiative under `docs/intent/`, treat docs and demo examples as part of each user-visible feature's scope. List the docs page or section and the example in the same milestone as the feature. The docs gate (`apps/demo/src/docs/gate.ts`) requires a page only for registered chart types; nothing enforces examples, Composition marks, or other features.
- Keep field names and filter state visible. Put optional actions beside or below the content.
- Prefer compact nonmodal popovers for inspection and settings. Keep the data visible during edits.
- In charts, a click on a mark selects it and a click on empty plot space clears that chart's filters. Marginals, legends, and overlays use the main marks' color encoding and visual treatment. See [UI defaults](docs/ui-defaults.md).
- Do not put "Inspect" buttons (Inspect records, Inspect point, Inspect bar, and the like) on charts or cards. Reach a chart trace by Alt-click or Alt-Enter on the mark, or from the trace control in the chart header.
- Start new imports with summary and rows. Preserve saved layouts.
- Quick previews must not create charts or change the saved layout.
- Use the shared `FieldMetadata` component in field lists and pickers.
- Verify changed flows in the browser at wide, intermediate, and narrow widths.
- Keep existing user edits. Do not commit on `main` without an explicit request.

## Verifying changes

- The demo dev server and demo tests run the library from source. Do not build the library to see a change; `pnpm --filter demo dev` picks it up. Set `EXPLOREDA_DIST=1` only to try the built package.
- While working, run targeted tests (`pnpm --filter exploreda exec vitest run <files>`) and `pnpm --filter exploreda check-types`.
- Take test time limits from `@repo/test-config`. Don't set a timeout in one test or one config; see [Test time limits](docs/agent-guide.md#test-time-limits).
- Run `pnpm check` once, before opening a PR. It builds the package, so never run it from several worktrees at the same time.

## Pull requests

- When carrying out an implementation plan, stack one PR per milestone. Open the first PR as soon as it builds, and keep going through every milestone without stopping to ask.
- Take screenshots with `pnpm shot`; see the [agent guide](docs/agent-guide.md#commands).
- Include screenshots in the PR description for every visible fix or UI change.
- Upload them with `gh pr create --attach` or `gh pr edit --attach`. Repeat the flag for each image and add alt text after `#`, as in `--attach 'tmp/shot.png#Rows drawer at 390px'`.
- To place an image beside the change it shows, reference the local file in the body, such as `![alt](./shot.png)`, and attach the same path. `gh` rewrites the reference to the uploaded URL.
- Keep screenshots under `tmp/`. Do not commit them, and do not push them to a separate assets branch.

## Changesets

- Every PR that changes what `exploreda` users get (anything under `packages/explorEDA` except tests, or its build output) includes a changeset. Write it yourself; nobody adds them later.
- Run `pnpm changeset:add <patch|minor|major> "summary"`. It writes `.changeset/<slug>.md` without prompts. Do not run the interactive `pnpm changeset`.
- Pick `patch` for fixes and polish, `minor` for new features or options, and `major` only when Byron asks for a breaking release. While the version is 0.x, a breaking API change is `minor`.
- Write the summary for package users: what they can now do or what now behaves differently, in one or two plain sentences. It goes straight into the changelog.
- One changeset per PR is usual. Edit it when the PR's scope changes instead of adding another.
- Skip it for demo-only, docs-only, test-only, or tooling-only PRs.
- Never run `changeset version` or `changeset publish`, and never edit versions or `CHANGELOG.md` by hand. The Release workflow does that through the "Version Packages" PR.
- Follow [the release steps](README.md#release). Use `release.yml` on `main` with npm trusted publishing. Do not add `NPM_TOKEN`.
- Run release checks on Node 24. Preserve Node's `AbortController` and `AbortSignal` in the demo test environment.
- Confirm the npm version, `latest` tag, provenance, and GitHub release after publishing. Check npm before retrying a partial failure.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: README.md

~~~~~~~~text
# explorEDA

![explorEDA icon and wordmark](apps/demo/public/brand/logo.svg)

**Embed an interactive analysis workspace in your React app.**

Give users linked charts, record-level tables, and editable calculated fields
without building the workspace around them.

[Live demo](https://exploreda.dev) ·
[Package docs](packages/explorEDA/README.md) ·
[Example source](apps/demo/src/demos)

![The order book example after selecting Web in Sales channels: a Channel: Web filter chip, 167 of 500 rows, and every chart narrowed to web orders.](apps/demo/public/landing/order-book-web.jpg)

## Why a workspace

Linked filtering alone is common. explorEDA also provides the parts around the
charts: configuration, record inspection, formulas, and restorable settings.

- **Linked views and their records.** A selection in one chart filters every
  other view and the record table, so users can check the rows behind a
  pattern.
- **Calculated fields users can inspect.** Formulas show their dependency
  chains, and the editor previews a draft before it is applied across views.
- **Settings your app keeps.** Users arrange the analysis visually. Your app
  receives the settings as JSON and restores them later through `savedData`.

Chart types include row, bar, line, scatter, 3D scatter, box plot, pivot
table, data table, summary table, metric card, markdown, and color legend.

Metric cards show a count, sum, or average for rows that match the chart
filters. Alt-click a card to see the inputs and excluded values.

Line Chart's **Calendar summaries** mode groups dated rows by UTC day, week,
or month. Select a period and series to filter linked views, then Alt-click
a point to see its calculation, date boundaries, and source rows.
Choose **Area** to fill each series from zero. **Stacked area** adds nonnegative
counts or sums. Incomplete periods break the stack. Traces show each band's
bounds and the series that establish its baseline.

Use **Split by** in Bar Chart to compare series within each category. Each
bar supports linked selection and source tracing for its category–series pair.
Choose **Stacked** to add counts or sums, or **100%** to compare nonnegative
shares. Tracing includes each category denominator and its source records.

Use **Size by** in Scatter Plot to draw bubbles with area proportional to a
nonnegative field. The size scale stays fixed during filtering. Click a bubble
to select its source row, or Alt-click it to see its size calculation.
Zero uses a hollow marker. Missing, invalid, and negative sizes remain available
through the source trace.

Choose **Density** in Scatter Plot to count numeric coordinate pairs in rectangular
bins. Set the X and Y bin counts, then select a bin to filter its exact source rows.
Alt-click a bin to see its intervals, counts, color scale, and source records. Bin edges
and the automatic color scale use all source rows, so linked filters keep a stable reference.

Choose **Map** to place rows by latitude and longitude in WGS 84 decimal degrees.
Add color and size fields, then click a point to select its source row.
Alt-click a point to see its source values, coordinate preparation, size, and projection.
Drag to pan, use **Fit data** for all source coordinates, or **Reset view** for the world.
Views persist in geographic units. Omitted and offscreen rows remain available in the trace.
The bundled [World Atlas land outline](https://github.com/topojson/world-atlas) supplies context without a tile service.

Choose **Region** in Map settings to join records to a GeoJSON FeatureCollection.
Import polygon geometry, choose the row and feature keys, and review the join preview.
Keys match by value and type. Choose count, sum, or average for the region metric.
Select a region to filter its records; Alt-click it to see its metric and contributors.
Alt-click outside the regions to list unmatched rows. Duplicate feature keys form one region without counting rows twice.
Patterns distinguish regions with no rows from regions with invalid measures. Zero remains a valid metric.
The color domain uses full-source bounds and stays fixed across filters and facets.
Geometry is saved once under `geometryAssets`; each chart stores its `geometryAssetId` reference.

Edit a chart where it is drawn. Double-click a chart or axis title to rename
it. Double-click a numeric axis to type its range, drag the axis to pan, or
drag one of its ends to stretch it. A range changes only the view: filters and
row counts stay as they were. Your app's `onStateChange` hears each edit once,
so one rename or one drag is one undo step.

## Install

```sh
pnpm add exploreda
```

React and ReactDOM 18 or 19 are peer dependencies.

## Use it in your React app

```tsx
import { ExplorEda, type SavedDataStructure } from "exploreda";
import "exploreda/dist/ExplorEda.css";

type Order = Record<string, string | number | boolean | null>;

export function OrdersExplorer({
  orders,
  savedSettings,
  onSettingsChange,
}: {
  orders: Order[];
  savedSettings?: SavedDataStructure;
  onSettingsChange: (settings: SavedDataStructure) => void;
}) {
  return (
    <ExplorEda
      data={orders}
      savedData={savedSettings}
      onStateChange={onSettingsChange}
    />
  );
}
```

Three props define the boundary between your app and the workspace:

| Prop            | Role     | What it does                                                                                                 |
| --------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `data`          | Input    | The rows your app supplies. Pass a new array when the rows change; in-place mutations are not observed.      |
| `savedData`     | Restore  | Optional settings that restore charts, calculations, Rows filters, and layout. Read on mount or replacement. |
| `onStateChange` | Callback | Called after meaningful edits with JSON settings, never raw rows. Your app decides where to keep them.       |

`savedData` is not a controlled value, so do not feed each `onStateChange`
result back into it. The [package docs](packages/explorEDA/README.md) cover
the settings shape, full analysis bundles with rows, and registering only the
charts you need.

## Before you integrate

- **Screen size:** desktop viewports of 1024 CSS pixels or more.
- **Browser:** DOM and Canvas 2D. The 3D scatter chart also needs WebGL.
- **Storage:** none built in. Settings and full analysis exports are JSON your
  app stores.

## Develop

This repository is a pnpm workspace: the library is in `packages/explorEDA`
and the demo site is in `apps/demo`. It requires pnpm 11.9.0.

```sh
pnpm install
pnpm check              # UI rules, build, typecheck, and tests
pnpm --filter demo dev  # demo site at http://localhost:5173/, library from source
```

## Local product grid

The explorEDA product grid and its images live in `pgm/data`. The original
download is `pgm/explorEDA.zip`. The viewer and CLI save edits in `pgm/data`.
They do not update the hosted project or the ZIP archive.

```bash
pnpm pgm:view                      # open the local viewer; Ctrl+C stops it
pnpm pgm project list --json       # inspect local projects
pnpm pgm node list --roots --compact --json
```

Run `pnpm install` first. These commands use the pinned npm release of
`@byronwallrus/product-grid-cli`, which includes the local viewer.
Commit changes in `pgm/data` to save local edits and images in Git.

## Release

Releases use [Changesets](https://github.com/changesets/changesets). Add a
changeset to any PR that changes the published package:

```sh
pnpm changeset:add patch "Keep bar tooltips inside the panel"
```

When a changeset reaches `main`, the Release workflow opens or updates a
"Version Packages" PR with the new version and changelog. Merging that PR
runs `pnpm check`, publishes `exploreda` to npm, and tags a GitHub release.
Commits without a changeset do not start a release.

The workflow uses npm trusted publishing through GitHub OIDC. It needs no
`NPM_TOKEN` secret. The npm connection uses these settings:

- Repository: `byronwall/explorEDA`
- Workflow filename: `release.yml`
- Environment: none
- Allowed actions: `npm publish` and `npm stage publish`

Keep `id-token: write` in the workflow permissions. Use Node 24, which includes
a compatible npm CLI. Trusted publishing requires npm 11.5.1 or later.

To start a release by hand:

1. Open **Actions → Release → Run workflow** and select `main`.
2. Review and merge the **Version Packages** PR when changesets are pending.
3. Wait for the publish run to pass. Check the npm version and GitHub release.

With no pending changesets, the manual run publishes any unpublished package
version already on `main`. It does not create a new version.

If a run fails, read its logs before retrying. Fix code or workflow problems
through a PR, then run Release again on `main`. Do not create another version
just to retry a failed publish. If publishing succeeded but a later step failed,
check npm before retrying.

Run `pnpm check` with Node 24 before changing the release runtime or test setup.
The demo test environment retains Node's `AbortController` and `AbortSignal`
because React Router uses Node's `Request`. Plain jsdom signals fail on Node 24.

npm metadata can lag after publishing. If the run reports success but the
version is missing, wait and check again. Confirm the `latest` tag and provenance
before calling the release complete.

## Inspiration

explorEDA was inspired by [DC.js](https://dc-js.github.io/dc.js/) and
[Crossfilter](https://github.com/crossfilter/crossfilter).
~~~~~~~~

~~~~~~~~text
import { examples } from "./examples";

export type ImplementationStatus =
  | "supported"
  | "not-supported"
  | "not-checked";
export type ReviewStatus = "reviewed" | "not-reviewed";
export type ExampleUsageStatus = "shown" | "reviewed" | "not-used";

type FeatureDefinition = {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  family: string;
  description: string;
  required: boolean;
  status: ImplementationStatus;
  gaps?: readonly string[];
  chartType?: string;
};

export type CoverageFeature = (typeof coverageFeatures)[number] &
  Pick<FeatureDefinition, "gaps">;
export type CoverageFeatureId = CoverageFeature["id"];
const typedCoverageFeatures: readonly CoverageFeature[] = coverageFeatures;
export type DemonstrationStatus = Extract<
  ExampleUsageStatus,
  "shown" | "reviewed"
>;

export type ExampleReview = {
  date: string;
  report: string;
  evidence: Partial<Record<CoverageFeatureId, string>>;
};

export type ExampleCoverage = {
  exampleId: string;
  intent: string;
  features: Partial<Record<CoverageFeatureId, DemonstrationStatus>>;
  review?: ExampleReview;
};

export const coverageFamilies = [
  ...new Set(coverageFeatures.map(({ family }) => family)),
] as [CoverageFeature["family"], ...CoverageFeature["family"][]];

const featureById = new Map(
  typedCoverageFeatures.map((feature) => [feature.id, feature] as const)
);
const exampleById = new Map<string, ExampleCoverage>(
  exampleCoverage.map((example) => [example.exampleId, example])
);

export function getExampleUsageStatus(
  featureId: CoverageFeatureId,
  exampleId: string
): ExampleUsageStatus {
  return exampleById.get(exampleId)?.features[featureId] ?? "not-used";
}

export function getImplementationStatus(
  featureId: CoverageFeatureId
): ImplementationStatus {
  return featureById.get(featureId)?.status ?? "not-checked";
}

export function getFeatureReviewStatus(
  featureId: CoverageFeatureId,
  manifest: readonly ExampleCoverage[] = exampleCoverage
): ReviewStatus {
  return manifest.some((example) => example.features[featureId] === "reviewed")
    ? "reviewed"
    : "not-reviewed";
}

export function getExamplesUsingFeature(
  featureId: CoverageFeatureId,
  manifest: readonly ExampleCoverage[] = exampleCoverage
): string[] {
  return manifest
    .filter((example) => example.features[featureId] !== undefined)
    .map((example) => example.exampleId);
}

export function getFeatureGapCount(featureId: CoverageFeatureId): number {
  const feature = featureById.get(featureId);
  return feature?.gaps?.length ?? 0;
}

export function getOpenGapCount(): number {
  return typedCoverageFeatures.reduce(
    (total, feature) => total + (feature.gaps?.length ?? 0),
    0
  );
}

export function findCoverageErrors(
  manifest: readonly ExampleCoverage[] = exampleCoverage,
  knownExampleIds: readonly string[] = examples.map(({ id }) => id)
): string[] {
  const errors: string[] = [];
  const knownExamples = new Set(knownExampleIds);
  const knownFeatures = new Set<string>(coverageFeatures.map(({ id }) => id));
  const declaredFeatures = new Set<string>();

  for (const entry of manifest) {
    if (!knownExamples.has(entry.exampleId)) {
      errors.push(`Unknown example: ${entry.exampleId}`);
    }
    for (const id of Object.keys(entry.features)) {
      if (!knownFeatures.has(id)) {
        errors.push(`Unknown feature: ${id}`);
      }
      declaredFeatures.add(id);
    }

    const reviewed = Object.entries(entry.features)
      .filter(([, status]) => status === "reviewed")
      .map(([id]) => id);
    if (reviewed.length > 0) {
      if (!/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(entry.review?.date ?? "")) {
        errors.push(`Missing review date: ${entry.exampleId}`);
      }
      if (!entry.review?.report?.startsWith("docs/reviews/")) {
        errors.push(`Missing review report: ${entry.exampleId}`);
      }
    }
    for (const id of reviewed) {
      if (!entry.review?.evidence[id as CoverageFeatureId]?.trim()) {
        errors.push(`Missing review evidence: ${entry.exampleId}/${id}`);
      }
    }
    for (const id of Object.keys(entry.review?.evidence ?? {})) {
      if (!reviewed.includes(id)) {
        errors.push(
          `Review evidence without reviewed assignment: ${entry.exampleId}/${id}`
        );
      }
    }
  }

  for (const feature of typedCoverageFeatures) {
    if (
      feature.required &&
      getImplementationStatus(feature.id) !== "not-supported" &&
      !feature.gaps?.length &&
      !declaredFeatures.has(feature.id)
    ) {
      errors.push(`Required feature has no example: ${feature.id}`);
    }
  }

  return errors;
}
~~~~~~~~

~~~~~~~~text
export interface ExampleDataset {
  /** Row count with its unit, such as "344 penguins". */
  rows: string;
  fields: number;
  source: "Real" | "Synthetic";
}

/** Capabilities a visitor can look for; each catalogue tab names its own. */
export const capabilities = {
  "Related tables":
    "Lookups across source tables, with each match and miss inspectable.",
  Maps: "Points placed by latitude and longitude, linked to the other charts.",
  "Density and bins":
    "Hexagons, binned counts, and smoothed density for many points.",
  Fits: "Linear, polynomial, and LOESS fits, per group and pooled.",
  Distributions:
    "Histograms, box and violin plots, and ECDFs compared by group.",
  "Time and calendars":
    "Daily to yearly series, stacked areas, and calendar heatmaps.",
  Flows: "Sankey flows from one category to the next.",
  Profiles: "Parallel coordinates: one line per row across several measures.",
  "Shares and mixes":
    "Stacked and 100% bars, heatmaps, and pivots of category mixes.",
  Calculations: "Calculated fields with formulas you can inspect and change.",
  "3D": "Rotatable 3D scatter plots with synchronized cameras.",
} as const;
export type Capability = keyof typeof capabilities;

/** A catalogue tab: its name in the workspace and what it shows. */
export interface ExampleTab {
  name: string;
  capabilities: Capability[];
}

export interface ExampleData {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  /** What the data is and why these views answer its question. */
  description: string;
  dataset: ExampleDataset;
  /** Workspace capabilities this example is built to show. */
  shows: string[];
  icon: LucideIcon;
  /** Listed in the catalogue. Other examples open only from their URL. */
  listed?: boolean;
  /** The tabs it opens with, for capability discovery. Listed examples only. */
  tabs?: ExampleTab[];
  /** A complete analysis: related tables from files, tabs from dashboard text. */
  analysis?: ExampleAnalysis;
  /** Dashboard text for a single-file example, with one `view` per tab. */
  text?: string;
  /** Related tables: the example opens a project instead of one file. */
  project?: AnalysisProject;
  tables?: Record<string, readonly AnalysisSourceRow[]>;
  queryPresets?: Record<string, SavedDataStructure>;
  /** The first tab's name, when the title does not fit a tab. */
  viewName?: string;
  data: string; // path to the data file
  savedData?: SavedDataStructure;
  /** More saved views that open as tabs beside the main one. */
  views?: ExampleView[];
}

const viewNames: Record<string, string> = {
  "3d-scatter": "3D scatter",
  bar: "histogram",
  calendar: "calendar",
  boxplot: "box plot",
  "color-legend": "legend",
  composition: "composition",
  waterfall: "waterfall",
  "data-table": "table",
  heatmap: "heatmap",
  ecdf: "ECDF",
  line: "line",
  markdown: "notes",
  "metric-card": "metric card",
  map: "map",
  "parallel-coordinates": "parallel coordinates",
  "scatter-matrix": "scatter matrix",
  pivot: "pivot",
  row: "row",
  sankey: "sankey",
  hierarchy: "hierarchy",
  scatter: "scatter",
  summary: "summary",
};

/** Counts saved views and names each chart type once, in layout order. */
export function describeViews(example: ExampleData) {
  const charts = example.savedData?.charts ?? [];
  const types = [...new Set(charts.map(({ type }) => viewNames[type] ?? type))];
  return {
    count: charts.length,
    types,
    tabs: 1 + (example.views?.length ?? 0),
  };
}

/** The catalogue: complete analyses, in the order the landing page lists them. */
export const catalogue = examples.filter((example) => example.listed);

/** The analysis the landing page features. */
export const FEATURED_ANALYSIS_ID = "january-flights";
~~~~~~~~

## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/gate.ts

~~~~~~~~text
import type { DocsPage } from "./catalogue";

/** Everything the docs gate checks, passed in so tests can break each part. */
export type DocsGateInput = {
  pages: readonly Pick<
    DocsPage,
    "topic" | "covers" | "chartType" | "examples" | "related" | "thumbnail"
  >[];
  /** Page source by topic, read from `pages/<topic>.mdx`. */
  sources: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  /** Asset file names in `assets/`. */
  assets: readonly string[];
  targets: readonly string[];
  exampleIds: readonly string[];
  /** The coverage features each example shows or has reviewed. */
  exampleFeatures: Readonly<Record<string, readonly string[]>>;
  /** Live examples by id: their example, data file, charts, and step charts. */
  liveExamples: Readonly<
    Record<
      string,
      {
        exampleId: string;
        data: string;
        charts: readonly string[];
        stepCharts: readonly string[];
      }
    >
  >;
  /** Files under `public/`, as URL paths such as `/datasets/x.csv`. */
  publicFiles: readonly string[];
  /** Every saved setting of each chart type, from the library. */
  settingKeys: Readonly<Record<string, readonly string[]>>;
  /** Chart types with an "At a glance" entry. */
  glanceTypes: readonly string[];
  /** Setting lines shared by every chart type. */
  commonDescriptions: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  /** Setting lines for one chart type. */
  chartDescriptions: Readonly<Record<string, Readonly<Record<string, string>>>>;
};

/**
 * Every way the docs can be wrong or incomplete, as messages that name the
 * page or target. An empty list means the docs may ship.
 */
export function findDocsErrors(input: DocsGateInput): string[] {
  const errors: string[] = [];
  const topics = new Set<string>();
  const targets = new Set(input.targets);
  const examples = new Set(input.exampleIds);
  const assets = new Set(input.assets);
  const covered = new Set<string>();

  for (const page of input.pages) {
    if (topics.has(page.topic)) {
      errors.push(`Two pages use the topic "${page.topic}"`);
    }
    topics.add(page.topic);
  }

  for (const page of input.pages) {
    const where = `Page "${page.topic}"`;
    if (!(page.topic in input.sources)) {
      errors.push(`${where} has no pages/${page.topic}.mdx`);
    }
    for (const target of page.covers) {
      if (!targets.has(target)) {
        errors.push(`${where} covers unknown target "${target}"`);
      }
      covered.add(target);
    }
    for (const topic of page.related) {
      if (!topics.has(topic)) {
        errors.push(`${where} links to missing page "${topic}"`);
      }
    }
    for (const id of page.examples) {
      if (!examples.has(id)) {
        errors.push(`${where} uses unknown example "${id}"`);
      } else if (
        page.chartType &&
        !input.exampleFeatures[id]?.includes(`chart:${page.chartType}`)
      ) {
        errors.push(
          `${where} uses example "${id}", which does not show a ${page.chartType} chart`
        );
      }
    }
  }

  for (const [topic, source] of Object.entries(input.sources)) {
    const where = `pages/${topic}.mdx`;
    if (!topics.has(topic)) {
      errors.push(`${where} has no catalogue entry`);
    }
    for (const [, linked] of source.matchAll(/<DocsLink\s+topic="([^"]+)"/g)) {
      if (linked && !topics.has(linked)) {
        errors.push(`${where} links to missing page "${linked}"`);
      }
    }
    for (const [, linked] of source.matchAll(/\]\(\/docs\/([^)#/]+)/g)) {
      if (linked && !topics.has(linked)) {
        errors.push(`${where} links to missing page "${linked}"`);
      }
    }
    for (const [, id] of source.matchAll(/\]\(\/examples\/([^)?#/]+)/g)) {
      if (id && !examples.has(id)) {
        errors.push(`${where} links to unknown example "${id}"`);
      }
    }
    for (const [, file] of source.matchAll(/from\s+"\.\.\/assets\/([^"]+)"/g)) {
      if (file && !assets.has(file)) {
        errors.push(`${where} imports missing image "${file}"`);
      }
    }
  }

  const live = new Set(Object.keys(input.liveExamples));
  const publicFiles = new Set(input.publicFiles);
  const referenced = new Set<string>();
  for (const [topic, source] of Object.entries(input.sources)) {
    const where = `pages/${topic}.mdx`;
    for (const [, id] of source.matchAll(/<LiveExample\s+id="([^"]+)"/g)) {
      if (id && !live.has(id)) {
        errors.push(`${where} uses missing live example "${id}"`);
      }
    }
    if (source.includes("<SettingsReference")) {
      errors.push(
        `${where} renders a settings reference; chart pages get theirs from the page template`
      );
    }
  }
  // Every chart page ends with its settings, and opens with its glance.
  for (const page of input.pages) {
    if (!page.chartType) {
      continue;
    }
    referenced.add(page.chartType);
    if (!input.glanceTypes.includes(page.chartType)) {
      errors.push(
        `Page "${page.topic}" has no entry in glance.ts for "${page.chartType}"`
      );
    }
  }
  for (const page of input.pages) {
    if (page.thumbnail && !live.has(page.thumbnail)) {
      errors.push(
        `Page "${page.topic}" uses missing live example "${page.thumbnail}" as its thumbnail`
      );
    }
  }
  // Dashboard text paths quoted on a chart page must be settings it saves.
  for (const page of input.pages) {
    const source = input.sources[page.topic];
    const keys = page.chartType ? input.settingKeys[page.chartType] : undefined;
    if (!source || !keys) continue;
    for (const [, path] of source.matchAll(/`([A-Za-z][\w.]*)=[^`]*`/g)) {
      const key = path?.split(".")[0];
      if (key && !keys.includes(key)) {
        errors.push(
          `pages/${page.topic}.mdx quotes "${path}=", but ${page.chartType} charts have no setting "${key}"`
        );
      }
    }
  }
  for (const [id, spec] of Object.entries(input.liveExamples)) {
    const where = `Live example "${id}"`;
    if (!examples.has(spec.exampleId)) {
      errors.push(`${where} opens unknown example "${spec.exampleId}"`);
    }
    if (!publicFiles.has(spec.data)) {
      errors.push(`${where} loads missing data "${spec.data}"`);
    }
    for (const chart of spec.stepCharts) {
      if (!spec.charts.includes(chart)) {
        errors.push(`${where} has a step on missing chart "${chart}"`);
      }
    }
  }
  for (const type of referenced) {
    const keys = input.settingKeys[type];
    if (!keys) {
      errors.push(`Settings reference for unknown chart type "${type}"`);
      continue;
    }
    for (const key of keys) {
      if (
        !input.chartDescriptions[type]?.[key] &&
        !input.commonDescriptions[key]
      ) {
        errors.push(`Setting "${type}.${key}" has no description`);
      }
    }
  }
  for (const [type, lines] of Object.entries(input.chartDescriptions)) {
    const keys = new Set(input.settingKeys[type] ?? []);
    for (const key of Object.keys(lines)) {
      if (!keys.has(key)) {
        errors.push(`Description for "${type}.${key}", which is not a setting`);
      }
    }
  }
  const everyKey = new Set(Object.values(input.settingKeys).flat());
  for (const key of Object.keys(input.commonDescriptions)) {
    if (!everyKey.has(key)) {
      errors.push(`Shared description for "${key}", which no chart saves`);
    }
  }

  for (const target of input.targets) {
    if (!covered.has(target)) {
      errors.push(`No page covers "${target}"`);
    }
  }
  return errors;
}

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/bar.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Fields and computation

Choose a field to group. The selected operation determines what each bar means.

<Terms
  items={[
    [
      "Category counts",
      "A categorical field counts rows in each category. Click a bar to filter.",
    ],
    [
      "Numeric bins",
      "A numeric field is binned by default. Drag across bins to filter a snapped range.",
    ],
    [
      "Grouped summary",
      "Count rows, or sum or average a measure field, for each group.",
    ],
  ]}
/>

## Try it

Drag across the delivery-time bins to filter the channel bars, or click a channel to see which delivery times it covers.

<LiveExample
  id="bar-histogram"
  caption="Delivery days in numeric bins beside orders by sales channel, from the order-book example."
/>

## Selection and row scope

Category-count bars use rows that remain after other filters and the current facet. Clicking a category filters linked views. Numeric bins use that same live row set; dragging across bins filters to their snapped numeric range. The count and bin domains use all source rows, which keeps categories and scale positions stable.

<Callout title="Grouped summaries read a different scope.">
  A named grouped summary calculates from globally filtered rows. When chart
  filters or facets are active, its row scope can differ from category counts.
</Callout>

## Settings and limits

Set the grouping field, choose category counts or numeric bins, and adjust the bin count. For a grouped summary, choose a group field, an operation, and—when needed—a numeric measure.

- Missing or non-finite measurements do not enter numeric bins.
- Alt-click a bar or use the chart trace action to inspect how it was computed.

## Series

**Split by** a second field to divide each bar into series.

- **Grouped** places series side by side to compare separate values.

<LiveExample
  id="bar-grouped"
  caption="Revenue by region with one bar per channel, from the grouped bars example."
/>

- **Stacked** adds series into one bar. **100%** shows each series as a share of its category total.

<LiveExample
  id="bar-stacked"
  caption="Orders by category split into channel shares, from the stacked bars example."
/>

## Annotations

The **Notes** tab adds lines and bands on the value axis, such as a goal. A **callout** attaches to a bar by its **Category**: a bar sums many rows, so it never stands for one record. Series bars group their marks, so they take lines and bands only.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/chart-settings.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Open chart settings

Use the settings button in a chart's header, or hover a chart and press <kbd>S</kbd>. The panel opens beside the chart and leaves it in view, so you see each change as you make it.

## Try it

Switch the histogram to category counts. The chart redraws at once, with one bar for each delivery time and one for the orders that have none.

<LiveExample
  id="settings-bar"
  caption="The delivery-time histogram beside its real settings panel."
/>

## Tabs

The tabs depend on the chart type:

- **Data** chooses the fields and what each mark counts or measures.
- **Fit** adds regression fits, on scatter plots.
- **Facets** splits a chart into small multiples by one or two fields.
- **Axes** sets each axis's scale, tick density, grid, and range.
- **Filters** edits the chart's selection by hand.
- **Labels** sets the title, subtitle, and source note, their type size and weight, and the axis titles.
- **Notes** adds callouts, lines, bands, regions, saved records, and supplied intervals that save with the chart, on scatter plots, line charts, bar and row charts, and box plots. See [Scatter plot](/docs/scatter#annotations) and [Line chart](/docs/line#annotations).
- **Spacing** sets the margins around the plot.

## Changes and Reset

Every valid edit applies right away. **Reset** undoes every change made since the panel opened, in one step. A range whose minimum passes its maximum is not applied until you fix it.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/color.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Shared color scales

Charts that color by the same field share its scale, so a category has one color across the dashboard.

- **Categorical** scales give each value its own color from a palette, such as Okabe Ito, Tableau 10, or the current theme's palette.
- **Numerical** scales map values onto a continuous color ramp.
- Open **Colors** in the toolbar to change a scale's palette and order. The panel warns when two colors look alike to readers with color vision deficiencies.

## Legends

A <DocsLink topic="color-legend">color legend</DocsLink> shows any shared scale on its own, so one key can explain several charts.

<LiveExample
  id="color-legend"
  caption="A color legend above box plots colored by the same scale, from the box plot example."
/>

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/composition.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Try it

<LiveExample
  id="composition"
  caption="A composed graphic built from the message log example."
/>

## How it reads

- Chart units draw marks from the rows, through named scales that every repeat shares.
- Text and labels can show values computed from the data.
- A single repeat can carry its own visual changes, kept apart from the template.

## Waterfall and donut marks

A chart unit can draw a waterfall or a donut in each repeat. They compute their values as the [Waterfall](/docs/waterfall) and [Donut](/docs/donut) charts do, so a report page and the workspace show the same numbers.

<LiveExample
  id="composition-parts"
  caption="Each quarter's plan-to-actual bridge and each budget group's shares, from the lab budget report example."
/>

- **Waterfall** bridges a start state to an end state with one signed step per category, or runs a total through ordered steps from a start value. Every repeat shares one value axis, so a quarter's deficit reads against the others. Steps can group under a field, with a hollow checkpoint for each subtotal, and the start and end draw as a line from zero to a dot.
- **Donut** shows each category's share of the repeat's rows, largest first, with the smaller categories pooled into Other. Categories keep one color across repeats, and a legend element lists them. A repeat with a negative part or no total shows no shares.
- Both trace like any mark: Alt-click a step or slice for its value, how it was calculated, and its rows.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/data-table.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Try it

<LiveExample id="data-table" caption="Orders, from the order-book example." />

## How it reads

- The table shows the rows that pass every other chart's filters.
- **Distributions in headers** draws each field's distribution under its column name. Click or drag a distribution to filter that column.

<Callout title="Search stays in the table.">
  Search and sorting change only what this table shows. To filter every other
  chart, turn on **Distributions in headers** and click or drag one.
</Callout>

## Sorting, filtering, and formatting

- Click a column heading to sort by it, and again to reverse the order.
- Right-click a heading or cell to filter, sort, move, hide, or copy.
- Drag a column by its name to reorder it, or drag it away from the header to hide it.
- Columns fit their names and values.
- Values follow each field's format settings, such as currency, units, and precision.
- The table draws only the rows in view, so long tables scroll quickly.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/facets.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Small multiples

Facets repeat one chart for each value of a field, so you can compare groups side by side.

- **Wrap** lays out one repeat per value of a row variable, in a set number of columns.
- **Grid** lays out repeats by a row variable and a column variable.
- **Visible facets** chooses which repeats show, and in what order.

Turn on facets in a chart's **Facets** settings.

An annotation draws in every panel unless its **Panel** setting in the **Notes** tab names one. A panel is named by its facet values, so reordering or hiding panels never moves a note to another group. A calculated line reads each panel's own rows, so each panel draws its own value.

## Shared scales

Every repeat uses the chart's axis domains, which come from all source rows. Repeats compare on the same scales, and their positions stay put as filters change.

<LiveExample
  id="facets"
  caption="The same scatter plot repeated for each sex, from the scatter regression example."
/>

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/filters.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## How filters work

A chart's selection is its filter. Click a bar, row, cell, or flow, or drag across a range, and every other chart narrows to the matching rows. A bar chart keeps showing its own unselected bars, so you can still see what you left out.

- The filter bar shows the row count and a chip for each filter.
- **Clear this chart's filters** in a chart's header, or <kbd>C</kbd>, clears one chart.
- **Clear all filters** in the filter bar clears every chart, table search, and Rows filter.
- A table's search and a Rows filter limit only their own table.
- A chart's local filter limits only the rows that chart draws. It never filters other charts.
- A workspace filter, added with **Filter** in the filter bar, narrows every chart without a chart of its own. It saves with the view.

## Saved filters

Filters save with the chart, so a dashboard can open with a selection already in place.

<LiveExample
  id="saved-filter"
  caption="The Lorenz example opens with a brush on Time and Z already drawn."
/>

## Related

See the <DocsLink topic="bar">bar chart</DocsLink> and <DocsLink topic="scatter">scatter plot</DocsLink> guides for how each chart reads its rows, and <DocsLink topic="rendering">How rendering works</DocsLink> for full, peer-filtered, and globally filtered rows.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/in-place-editing.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Edit where it's drawn

- Double-click a chart title to edit it. Enter, Tab, or leaving the field keeps the change; Escape restores the title it opened with.
- Double-click a numeric axis to set its range. Leave a side blank to follow the data.
- Drag along a numeric axis to pan its range, or drag an end to stretch it.

<Callout type="warning" title="A range hides marks; it doesn't filter rows.">
  Marks outside an axis range are not drawn, but their rows still count
  everywhere else. To narrow every chart, select instead.
</Callout>

## One edit, one step

Each edit is reported once, when it ends: a typed title, an axis drag, or a session in the axis range popover. A host that records each change as an undo step gets one step per edit. An edit cancelled with Escape is not reported.

## Try it

<LiveExample
  id="bar-histogram"
  caption="Double-click the histogram's title or its X axis to edit them in place."
/>

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/line.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Try it

<LiveExample
  id="line"
  caption="Monthly revenue by sales channel, from the calendar series example."
/>

## How it reads

- With a date on the X axis, the chart summarizes rows into periods. Choose the interval, the week start, and the metric: a count, or a summary of a measure.
- **Display** draws a line between period values, an area filled to zero, or stacked areas that add nonnegative counts or sums across series.
- **Missing periods** either leave a gap, or draw an empty count or sum at zero. An average needs observations, so an empty period has no average to draw.
- Each series can use the right axis, its own line style, and points.

## Areas

<LiveExample
  id="line-area"
  caption="Weekly order counts as filled areas, from the area charts example."
/>

## Annotations

The **Notes** tab marks what the lines mean:

- A **line** at a Y value marks a target. On a date axis, a line at a date marks an event, such as a release.
- A **band** shades a span of Y values, such as a tolerance, or a period of dates or X values. **Bracket** marks the span along the top edge instead of shading the plot.
- A **callout** labels one row of a line chart by its key and the series it sits on. A chart that sums rows into periods has no single rows, so its callouts sit at a fixed date and value.
- An **interval** shades between lower and upper bounds that each row already holds, such as a supplied forecast range. Its **Meaning** says what the bounds are; the chart computes no interval itself. Rows whose lower bound is above the upper bound are drawn with the bounds swapped.

Regions, saved records, extreme callouts, and calculated lines work as they do on a [scatter plot](/docs/scatter#annotations), on line charts that draw rows. On a chart with a right axis, choose which axis a value reads on. The product activity example marks the release period and a 4% target; the calendar example brackets a summer sale.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/map.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Try it

<LiveExample
  id="map"
  caption="Service sites as points, from the point map example."
/>

## How it reads

- **Points** use the coordinates in each row. Points can be sized by a field and labeled.
- **Regions** join rows to polygon keys and summarize a measure for each region. Pick World countries (joined by ISO alpha-3, alpha-2, or numeric code), US states (postal or FIPS code), or US counties (FIPS code), or import your own GeoJSON. The feature key must match the row key exactly, in value and type.
- **Picking a level**: switching a map to Region mode looks for a column of country, state, or county codes and starts on the matching boundaries. Zoom a country map into the US and it shows states, then counties, when the data has those columns. A `region()` calculation can add them from coordinates.
- **Layers**: the layers button under the map hides or shows the points, writes point labels beside them (up to 150 points), draws reference outlines such as states or districts beneath the points, and switches the basemap between automatic, outline only, and none. Outlines are not selectable, and hiding points keeps the selection.
- **Crowded maps**: every point is drawn by default, so global patterns stay visible. Turn on Group in the layers popover to draw, above 2,000 points, counted circles in the most common color of their points; zoom in to split them, and click one to select its rows.
- **Images**: the image button saves the map as a PNG at twice its size, with the street basemap and its credits when one is showing.
- **Selecting**: click a point or region to filter other charts. Click empty map space or press Escape to clear this map's filters. Alt-click traces.
- **Projection**: Equal Earth preserves relative areas. Equirectangular places longitude and latitude on straight, evenly spaced lines.
- The zoom and center are saved, and the view refits when the chart is resized.

## Street maps

Point maps can draw streets and place names once you zoom past continent scale. Wide views keep the Equal Earth outline, so world comparisons keep honest areas. Panning and zooming keep the same selection, and the chart saves the view.

Street maps are optional. Install `maplibre-gl` (version 4 or 5), then register it once:

```tsx
import maplibregl from "maplibre-gl";
import "maplibre-gl/dist/maplibre-gl.css";
import { registerMapLibre } from "exploreda";

registerMapLibre(maplibregl);
```

The default basemap is OpenFreeMap: free, with no key, in light and dark styles that follow the workspace. Pass `{ styles: { light, dark } }` to use another provider. If the style cannot load, the map shows the outline with a short notice, and selection keeps working.

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/scales-and-axes.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Scales

- **Linear** uses evenly spaced values. It is the standard scale for most charts.
- **Symmetric log** shows detail across large ranges while keeping zero and negative values. Choose it in a chart's **Axes** settings.
- **Log**: each tenfold step takes the same space. Scatter plots offer it on both axes, line charts on their value axis when drawn as lines, and box plots on their value axis. Rows at or below zero can't be placed; the axis counts them. An ECDF can also plot X on a log scale, and composition scales can use log spacing.
- **Time**: line charts summarize dates into UTC days, weeks, months, or years.
- **Band**: category fields sit in evenly spaced bands, as in row and bar charts and box plots.

## Ticks, grids, and ranges

- Tick density sets about how many tick values an axis shows, from 2 to 12.
- Grid lines can be turned on or off for each axis.
- An axis range sets the values it shows. Leave a side blank to follow the data. Marks outside are hidden, not filtered.

## Axis titles

Set each axis title and its units in a chart's **Labels** settings. A blank title uses the field's label.

## Try it

<LiveExample
  id="settings-bar"
  caption="Open the Axes tab to change the scale, tick density, grid, and range."
/>

~~~~~~~~


## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/pages/scatter.mdx

~~~~~~~~text
## Fields and computation

Choose one field for X and one for Y. Each plotted point represents a source row with finite positions. In the `scatter-trace` example, both axes are calculated fields: Net sales is X and Contribution is Y.

<CalcChain
  rows={[
    { name: "Gross sales", expression: "Units × Unit Price" },
    { name: "Net sales", expression: "Gross sales − Discount" },
    { name: "Contribution", expression: "Net sales − Cost" },
  ]}
/>

## Try it

Click a channel to narrow the points to its orders. Drag across the plot to brush a range on both axes.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter"
  caption="Calculated net sales against contribution for 18 orders, from the scatter trace example."
/>

## Selection and row scope

Drag across the plot to brush a rectangular range on both axes. The brush filters linked views. Other active filters also limit the visible rows. The axes keep their domains from the full source population, so the scale stays stable as rows are filtered.

<Callout title="Trace a point.">
  Use the chart trace action or Alt-click a point to inspect its source values,
  calculations, filters, scales, and position. Trace works on every chart type
  except 3D scatter plots, data tables, pivot tables, summary tables, and notes.
</Callout>

## Settings and limits

Choose X, Y, and optional color fields. Adjust point size and opacity to make overlap easier to see.

- On numeric axes, rows with missing or non-finite values have no point. Categorical axes can include a missing-value category.
- Canvas draws the points; SVG draws the axes and the brush.
- The rectangular brush selects ranges on both axes.

## Displays for many points

**Display** in the Data tab changes what the plot draws when points overlap.

- **Density** counts coordinate pairs in rectangular bins, shaded by count.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter-density"
  caption="10,000 daily observations in rectangular bins, from the scatter density example."
/>

- **Hexbin** counts pairs in hexagons. Points can show underneath.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter-hexbin"
  caption="Hexagon bins, from the scatter surfaces example."
/>

- **Contour** estimates a smoothed density and draws filled regions and contour lines, with bandwidth and level controls.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter-contour"
  caption="Smoothed density, from the scatter surfaces example."
/>

## Fits

The **Fit** tab draws a regression for each color group in each facet: linear, polynomial, or LOESS, with an optional overall fit through every group.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter-regression"
  caption="A linear fit per species, from the scatter regression example."
/>

## Bubbles

**Size by** a numeric field to draw bubbles. Bubble area stays proportional to value, and the largest radius is set in pixels.

<LiveExample
  id="scatter-bubble"
  caption="Points sized by a value, from the bubble scatter example."
/>

## Annotations

The **Notes** tab adds annotations that save with the chart.

- A **callout** is a label on one record. It finds the record by a key field, such as `Day`, whose values identify every row once. Reordered or replaced rows keep the callout on the same record. If no row has the key, or more than one does, the list says so and the chart draws nothing for it.
- A callout can instead sit at a **fixed point**, given in X and Y values. **Arrowhead** ends its leader line with an arrow at the subject.
- A **line** marks a fixed X or Y value, such as a target, and a **band** shades or brackets a span between two values. Filters never move them.
- A callout set to **Extreme** follows whichever row has the largest or smallest value, so its subject can change as filters change.
- A **region** is a rectangle or ellipse in X and Y values. It means "this area": filtering changes which points fall inside it.
- **Saved records** copies the rows your current selection holds, by key. It means "these records": the squares stay on the same rows when the selection changes, and the label counts how many show.
- A line's **Value from** can be **Calculated**: the mean, median, minimum, or maximum of the axis field over **All rows** (every row the chart can draw) or **Filtered rows** (those that pass the current filters). Its label names the rows and shows the result.
- Each annotation has a name, and **Duplicate** copies it below.

Drag a callout's label, or focus it and use the arrow keys, to move the label without moving its record. Each drag is one undo step, and Escape puts the label back. When filters leave out a callout's record, the callout hides and the list marks it **Filtered out** until the record returns. An annotation past the axis range stays in the list as **Outside the view**.

The bubble scatter example has a callout on day 31, a conversion target, a mean response time over filtered rows, and a region around the fastest days.

~~~~~~~~

~~~~~~~~text
</Callout>
~~~~~~~~

## Source: apps/demo/src/docs/reference/settingDescriptions.ts

~~~~~~~~text
/**
 * One line for each saved chart setting, shown in each chart page's settings
 * reference. Keys come from the library, so the docs gate fails when a
 * documented chart type gains a setting with no line here, or keeps a line
 * for a setting it no longer has.
 */

/** Settings every chart type shares. A chart type may describe one its own way. */
export const COMMON_SETTINGS: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {
  title: "Title in the chart header.",
  subtitle: "Line under the title that says what the chart shows.",
  note: "Source or note line under the plot.",
  style: "Title and subtitle type that differs from the workspace theme.",
  layout: "Position and size on the dashboard grid, in grid columns and rows.",
  facet:
    "Small multiples by one field (wrap) or two (grid), on chart types that offer facets.",
  filters: "The chart's selection. It filters every other chart.",
  localFilters:
    "Limits the rows this chart draws. Unlike its selection, it never filters other charts.",
  margin: "Space around the plot, in pixels, on charts that draw a plot.",
  xAxis:
    "X axis scale type, tick and label sizes, grid, and range limits, on charts with an X axis.",
  yAxis:
    "Y axis scale type, tick and label sizes, grid, and range limits, on charts with a Y axis.",
  xAxisLabel:
    "X axis title, on charts with an X axis. Blank uses the field's label.",
  yAxisLabel:
    "Y axis title, on charts with a Y axis. Blank uses the field's label.",
  xGridLines:
    "About how many tick values the X axis shows, from 2 to 12, on charts with numeric ticks.",
  yGridLines:
    "About how many tick values the Y axis shows, from 2 to 12, on charts with numeric ticks.",
  colorField:
    "Field that colors the marks, on charts that color by a field. Charts that color by the same field share its colors.",
  colorScaleId:
    "The color scale for the color field, shared by name across charts.",
  annotations:
    "Saved callouts, lines, bands, regions, saved records, and supplied intervals, on charts that draw annotations. A callout on a record finds it by key field values, never by row position. Date values are UTC timestamps in milliseconds.",
};

/** Settings described for one chart type. */
export const CHART_SETTINGS: Readonly<
  Record<string, Readonly<Record<string, string>>>
> = {
  row: {
    field: "Category field; each value gets its own row.",
    minRowHeight:
      "Smallest row height in pixels. When rows would be shorter, the chart shows fewer categories.",
    maxRowHeight: "Largest row height in pixels, so a few rows stay compact.",
    aggregateId:
      "Grouped summary to rank instead of counting rows. Its group field must match `field`.",
    topN: "Number of categories to show, with the rest gathered in Other. Leave unset to fit as many rows as the height allows.",
  },
  line: {
    field: "Not read by this chart type.",
    xField: "Field on the X axis: a date or a number.",
    seriesField: "Fields drawn as lines, one line each.",
    seriesSettings:
      "Per-line style by field: points, line width and opacity, color, dash, and the right axis.",
    styles: "Line shape between points: straight, smooth (monotone), or steps.",
    time: "Date summary: line, area, or stacked area; the interval and week start; the metric and measure; a split field; and whether missing periods leave a gap or draw zero.",
    showLegend: "Shows the legend.",
    legendPosition: "Where the legend sits: top, right, bottom, or left.",
    showXGrid:
      "Older grid switch. When yAxis.grid is unset, it decides the Y axis grid.",
    showYGrid:
      "Older grid switch. When xAxis.grid is unset, it decides the X axis grid.",
  },
  boxplot: {
    field: "Numeric field each box summarizes.",
    colorField:
      "Field that groups the rows into one box per value, each in its own color.",
    whiskerType:
      "How far whiskers reach: 1.5 times the interquartile range (Tukey), the minimum and maximum, or standard deviations.",
    showOutliers: "Shows values beyond the whiskers as separate points.",
    showObservations:
      "Shows individual values over each box: the first 300 valid rows per group.",
    violinOverlay: "Adds a density shape around each box.",
    violinBandwidth:
      "Density smoothing for the violin, when it is not automatic.",

/** A setting's line for a chart type, or undefined when it has none. */
export function describeSetting(type: string, key: string) {
  return CHART_SETTINGS[type]?.[key] ?? COMMON_SETTINGS[key];
}
~~~~~~~~

## Source: docs/agent-guide.md

~~~~~~~~text
# Agent guide

A map of the code and the commands that work, for agents changing this repo. Read it before a broad search; most tasks touch a few known files. Rules live in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) and [UI defaults](ui-defaults.md).

## Where things live

| Concern                                                   | Start here                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Store: rows, charts, filters, save/restore                | `packages/explorEDA/src/providers/DataLayerProvider.tsx` (zustand). `updateChart(id, partial)` is the one write path for chart settings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Host callback                                             | `onStateChange` in the same file. It fires after every store change that alters `saveToStructure()`, unless an in-place edit holds it (`holdStateChanges`). It is not debounced.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Settings types                                            | `src/types/ChartTypes.ts` (`BaseChartSettings`, `AxisSettings`), `src/types/SavedDataStructure.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Defaults                                                  | `src/utils/defaultSettings.ts`. `DEFAULT_AXIS_SETTINGS` writes `min: 0, max: 100` into every axis. Those keys are a dead placeholder, marked `@deprecated`; axis ranges live in `xAxis.limits` and `yAxis.limits`. Never give `min` or `max` meaning.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Saved-data validation                                     | `src/utils/saveDataUtils.ts` (`isAxis`, `validateSavedData`). A new setting that fails validation makes restores reject the whole save.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Chart registry                                            | `src/charts/registry.ts`, `registerAllCharts.ts`, and one `src/components/charts/<Type>/definition.ts` per type                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Chart panel: header, title, actions, details dialog, keys | `src/components/PlotChartPanel.tsx`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Grid placement and drag                                   | `src/components/ChartGridLayout.tsx` (react-grid-layout, `draggableHandle=".drag-handle"`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Settings popover and tabs                                 | `src/components/ChartSettingsContent.tsx`, `src/components/settings/*Tab.tsx`. Valid edits apply live; Reset restores the values from when the popover opened.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Axes                                                      | `components/charts/Axis/axisPlan.ts` plans ticks, labels, and guides. `AxisLayer.tsx` draws them (`PlannedAxes`, `PlannedGrid`). `numericScale.ts` makes linear and symlog scales.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| SVG chart shell                                           | `components/charts/BaseChart.tsx`: plot clip, brush, guide tracing, empty-plot clicks. Scatter draws its own `ScatterPlot/ScatterSvg.tsx`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Domains                                                   | Each renderer builds its own scale: `BarChart/barPlan.ts`, `seriesBarPlan.ts`, `ScatterPlot/scatterAxis.ts`, `LineChart/timeSeriesPlan.ts`, `RowChart.tsx`, `BoxPlot.tsx`. Grep `numericScale(` to find them all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Chart annotations (callouts, lines, bands, regions)       | `src/lib/annotations/`: saved types, `resolve.ts` (record keys and projection), `validate.ts`. `context.ts` says what a chart offers (dates, records, right axis). A renderer builds an `AnnotationFrame` and passes `annotationSlot(settings, frame)` to `ScatterSvg` or `BaseChart`; `AnnotationLayer.tsx` draws and drags them and reports statuses (`annotationStatus.ts`) back to the Notes tab in `settings/annotations/`. A record callout stores key field values, never a row index; aggregate charts attach callouts by `category`. Facet panels pass `facetKey`, the semantic facet id, for annotations bound to one panel. `scaleFrame` builds a frame from D3 scales. `chartDrawsAnnotations` lists the charts that draw them; `persistence.test.ts` round-trips every kind through Dashboard text and saved data. |
| Tracing (Alt-click)                                       | `components/charts/trace/` (`ChartTraceScope`, `useTraceSource`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Axis field menu and Command-click inspect                 | `components/charts/AxisFieldActions.tsx`. It acts on elements with `data-field`, and on axis bands when the panel passes `axisItems` (range and title actions).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Dashboard text (DSL)                                      | `src/lib/dsl/compile.ts` (keys such as `x.min`, `y.scale`), `export.ts` (diffs settings into keys generically), `settingKeys.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Demo history and undo                                     | `apps/demo/src/savedViewsHistory.ts` (`pushCheckpoint`), `SavedViewsWorkspace.tsx` (`capture`). Every `onStateChange` becomes one undo step.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| How the demo loads the library                            | `apps/demo/exploredaSource.ts`. Dev and demo tests use library source; set `EXPLOREDA_DIST=1` for the build.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| In-place editing                                          | `components/charts/InPlace/`. `useChartEdit` applies through `updateChart` and holds `onStateChange` until the edit ends: one edit, one undo step, and Escape reports nothing. `useTitleEditing` handles chart titles; `useAxisEditing` handles axis ranges, axis titles, axis drag, and menu items. `InlineTextEditor` and `AxisLimitFields` are the shared inputs, and `axisDrag.ts` holds the pure pan and stretch math.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Axis limits                                               | `components/charts/Axis/axisBounds.ts`: `boundedDomain(auto, axis)` applies `limits` over a renderer's automatic domain; `numericAxes(settings)` says which axes can take limits. Call it wherever a renderer builds a numeric domain, and skip `.nice()` when `hasAxisBounds`. `hiddenByBounds(axis, domain, values, unit)` counts what a bound hides; pass it as `hidden` to `planAxes`, and `PlannedAxes` draws the marker that `useHiddenNotice` opens.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Axis bands                                                | `AxisLayer.tsx` draws an invisible `.eda-axis-edit` group per numeric axis, under the tick labels. It carries `data-axis-edit`, `data-domain`, `data-range`, and `data-scale-type`, which the editors read and invert.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Docs site (`/docs/<topic>`)                               | `apps/demo/src/docs/`. Pages are MDX in `pages/`, listed in `catalogue.ts` with the targets they `covers`. `targets.ts` derives what must be documented (registered chart types, required `coverage.ts` features, flows, surfaces). `live/examples.ts` defines each live example (a focused layout from a demo dashboard) and its scripted steps. Settings tables come from the unpublished `exploreda/docs-kit` entry (`packages/explorEDA/src/docsKit/`) plus `reference/settingDescriptions.ts`. `gate.ts` fails on any uncovered target, broken link, unknown example, or undescribed setting; `pnpm --filter demo build` runs it first. `pnpm --filter demo docs:verify` (Playwright Test, `apps/demo/e2e/`) runs one test per page: every example's steps, the screenshots narrow screens show, and overflow at narrow widths. It runs in PR CI and in the Pages deploy before upload.     |
| Initiatives                                               | `docs/intent/<slug>/`: `intent-brief.md`, `initiative-map.json`, and when planned, `implementation-plan.md`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Work items and feedback                                   | Product Grid in `pgm/data`, read and written with the `pgm` CLI through `pnpm -s pgm <command> --json 2>/dev/null`.
~~~~~~~~
